115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品等進口代徵稅額增加，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額 534 億 元 ， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 10 月 13 日
上週重點訊息
◼ TOPIX 啟動大規模改革：日本交易所集團 10 月 7 日公布 TOPIX 調整結果，符合新制納入標
準的成分股將由 9 月底的 1,636 檔降至 986 檔，另有 35 檔股票新納入。現有成分股中，共
有 683 檔未符合流動性及浮動市值等標準，這些股票將自 2026 年 10 月底起逐步降低權
重，並於 2027 年 10 月重新評估，屆時仍未達標者將於 2028 年 7 月底降至零。此次改革有
助於改善 TOPIX 成分股的流動性及投資價值，並促使企業更加重視市場評價。

◼ 巴西總統選舉波索納洛首輪領先魯拉：巴西最高選舉法院 10 月 5 日公布首輪投票結果，參
議員弗拉維奧・波索納洛取得 47.03%的有效票，現任總統魯拉獲得 45.16%，兩人均未取得
過半票數，將於 10 月 25 日進行第二輪決選。若最終由波索納洛當選將相較有利於巴西股市
表現。
◼ IEA 支持加速釋出既有儲油：國際能源署（IEA）10 月 7 日表示，會員國支持加快釋出 3 月
已承諾提供的儲油，並將優先增加柴油供應。截至目前，會員國已釋出約 3.25 億桶儲油；若
剩餘承諾全數執行，可再向市場提供約 1 億桶供應，有助緩解運輸業與農業面臨的燃料成本
壓力。隨各國持續釋出戰略儲備石油，將有利於在短期內壓抑全球油價進一步上行。
市場展望
◼ 標普 500 獲利預估連續上修，資訊科技為主要推升來源：標普 500 企業獲利預估連續兩季上
修，其中資訊科技產業為主要改善來源，反映 AI、雲端運算及資料中心需求仍然強勁。雖然
美國資料中心仍面臨電力與基礎設施瓶頸，但建置規模持續擴大，AI 投資也逐步延伸至記憶

體、網路設備、資安及企業應用等領域。因此，預期科技類股的獲利動能仍具支撐，具 AI 供
應鏈優勢的台股與日股亦可望延續相關成長動能。
◼ 中天期債券可望兼顧息收與利率風險：美國經濟仍具韌性，加上政府與企業發債供給維持高
檔，可能使市場利率維持在較高水準。雖然投資級債信用利差位於相對低檔，但企業獲利、
現金流及償債能力仍具支撐，較高的美國公債基準利率也使其利息收益維持一定水準。短天
期債券面臨利率下降後的再投資風險，長天期債券則較易受到公債供給及利率波動影響，因
此中天期美國公債及投資級債可望在息收與利率風險之間維持較為平衡的表現。

2026年 11月 16日 全7頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 7-9月期 GDP（1次速報） 
個人消費の弱さと外需の下押しで、4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長に

経済調査部 チーフエコノミスト 神田 慶司 
  エコノミスト 畑中 宏仁

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）と小幅なが
らも 4 四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となった。設備投資が増加した一方、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少し、輸出減と輸入増から外需も成長率を押し下げた。在庫変動を除
く実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％と低迷するなど、内容は悪い。 
◼ 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と小幅ながらもプラス成長に転
じると見込んでいる。個人消費は小幅に増加し、設備投資も増加するだろう。輸出は
米国向けを中心に増加するものの、輸入の伸びが上回り、外需の寄与度は 2四半期連
続のマイナスになるとみている。リスク要因は AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化
であり、輸出や個人消費などへの影響に注意が必要だ。

※当社は、11月 20日（金）に「第231回日本経済予測」の発表を予定している。

実質GDPは小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長 
実質GDP成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と市場予想を下回り、内容も悪い 
2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP成長率は、1次速報値で前期比年率▲0.1％（前期比▲0.0％）だ
った。小幅ながらも 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となり、市場予想（QUICK調査中央値、前期
比年率＋0.2％）を下回った。なお、12月 8日に公表される 2次速報では 2025年度国民経済計
算年次推計の反映により、幅広い需要項目が改定される可能性がある。 
設備投資が増加に転じ、実質雇用者報酬の増加が続くなど緩やかな景気の拡大基調は続いて
いるとみられるが、在庫変動を除いた実質最終需要は前期比年率▲2.1％であり、個人消費が 2
四半期連続で減少するなど内容は悪い。 
また、交易利得・損失は7-9月期で▲11.4兆円（年率換算額）で1、実質GDPに交易利得・損
失を加えた実質 GDI（国内総所得）成長率は前期比年率▲1.6％だった。原油高などによる交易
条件の悪化で所得が海外に流出し、交易損失は前期から 2.2兆円拡大した。国内の所得環境は
GDPで見る以上に悪化したが、政府のエネルギー高対策が家計や企業への影響を一定程度緩和
した。 
実質 GDPを需要項目別に見ると（図表 2）、民需関連では個人消費・住宅投資が減少した一方、
設備投資は増加した。公需関連では政府消費と公共投資が減少した。公的在庫変動については、
7-9月期に大規模な石油の国家備蓄放出が行われなかったため、実質 GDP成長率への寄与度は
前期比＋0.5％pt と成長率を大きく押し上げた。外需関連では輸出減と輸入増により、純輸出
は実質GDP成長率を押し下げた。 
図表2：実質 GDP成長率と需要項目別の寄与度

（出所）内閣府統計より大和総研作成

1 交易条件（＝輸出デフレーター/輸入デフレーター）の変化による実質所得の増減で、プラスの場合は「交易 
利得」、マイナスの場合は「交易損失」。公表値は 2020暦年連鎖価格のデフレーターから算出されているた 
め（基準年の交易利得・損失額はゼロ）、交易利得・損失額はあくまでも基準年からの交易条件の変化の影響 
である点に留意が必要。

### Page 3

1

3 / 7

GDPデフレーターは前年同期比＋2.4％と 19四半期連続のプラスだった。また、単位労働コ
スト（＝名目雇用者報酬÷実質 GDP）は同＋4.4％と 14四半期連続のプラスで、賃金面からの
物価上昇圧力は足元でも継続している（巻末の関連指標①下段左）。

個人消費は、4-6月期が下方修正された上で 2四半期連続の減少 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と 2四半期連続で減少した。公表前の 4-6月期の個人消費は同＋
0.0％だったが、インバウンド消費額が上方修正されたことで同▲0.3％へと引き下げられた2。
7-9月期は一部耐久財の反動減や、9 月の天候不順といった一時的な下押し要因があったもの
の、所得環境が改善する中で 4-6月期から 2四半期連続で減少したところに、個人消費の弱さ
が見られる。 
国内家計最終消費支出を財・サービス別に見ると、耐久財（前期比▲3.5％）や半耐久財
（同▲1.4％）、非耐久財（同▲0.2％）がいずれも減少した一方、サービス（同＋0.1％）は増
加した（巻末の関連指標①上段左）。内閣府によると、耐久財ではエアコンなどが、半耐久財
では衣服などが、非耐久財ではその他個人ケア用器具（歯ブラシなど）などが押し下げたとい
う。4-6月期には、自動車税の環境性能割廃止や、2027年 4月に予定されているエアコンの省
エネ基準引き上げを見越した買い替え需要が耐久財消費を後押ししたが、その効果がはく落し
た。衣料品などの不振は天候不順が影響したとみられる。一方、サービスでは教育や携帯電話
通信料などが押し上げたという。 
実質雇用者報酬（家計最終消費支出デフレーターで実質化）は前期比＋0.3％と6四半期連続
で増加した（巻末の関連指標①上段右）。労働力調査（総務省）に見る雇用者数は同 0.0％で、
雇用者数で除した1人あたり実質雇用者報酬は同＋0.3％と6四半期連続で増加した（前年同期
比では3四半期連続のプラス）。

設備投資は3四半期ぶりの増加、住宅投資は2四半期連続の減少 
設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と 3四半期ぶりに増加した。建設投資は減少したものの、4-6月期
に特殊要因で減少していた研究開発投資で反動増が表れたほか、機械投資が増加したようだ。
内閣府によると、機械投資はサーバーや半導体製造装置などが増加に寄与したという。 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。原材料費高騰による建設資材価格の上
昇や人件費の上昇が重しとなっており、住宅着工戸数は 6月にかけて増加していたものの、7
月以降は下落に転じている（巻末の関連指標②上段右）。

2 家計最終消費支出は国内消費支出から非居住者家計の国内での直接購入（インバウンド消費）を控除して算
出されているため、インバウンド消費の上方修正は家計最終消費支出の下方修正につながる。

### Page 4

1

4 / 7

公共投資は 2四半期連続の減少、政府消費は6四半期ぶりに減少 
公共投資は前期比▲1.1％と2四半期連続で減少した。資材価格の高騰や人手不足などが重し
になっている上、2025年度補正予算に盛り込まれた公共事業の執行が一巡したとみられる。 
政府消費は前期比▲0.0％と小幅ながらも6四半期ぶりに減少した。内閣府によると、医療・
介護費が増加したものの、中間消費を中心にデフレーターが上昇したことが実質成長率の押し
下げにつながったという。 
また、実質 GDP成長率に対する公的在庫変動の寄与度は、7-9月期は大規模な石油の国家備
蓄放出が行われなかったため前期比＋0.5％ptとなり、成長率を大幅に押し上げた。後述のよ
うに原油の代替調達の進展によって、輸入の増加が実質 GDP成長率を押し下げたが、公的在庫
変動の増加がおおむね相殺した形だ。

輸出減と輸入増により、外需寄与度は前期比▲0.6％pt 
輸出は前期比▲0.2％と4四半期ぶりに減少した。財は増加したが、サービスが減少した。内
閣府によると、財では半導体製造装置や石油製品、基礎化学製品などが増加したという。サー
ビスでは、4-6月期に特殊要因で大幅に増加していた研究開発サービスに加え、インバウンド
や産業財産権等使用料なども減少したようだ。 
輸入は前期比＋2.5％と 2 四半期ぶりに増加した。サービスは減少したものの、財が増加し
た。財では、中東情勢の影響により 4-6月期に減少していた石油・天然ガスが代替調達の進展
によって増加したほか、非鉄金属やサーバー等も増加したようだ。サービスでは、アウトバウ
ンドは増加したものの、内閣府によると著作権等使用料や研究開発サービス、専門・経営コン
サルティングサービスなどが減少したという。 
輸出の減少と輸入の増加により、純輸出（外需）の実質 GDP成長率に対する寄与度は前期比
▲0.6％ptと、4四半期ぶりのマイナスとなった。

10-12月期の実質 GDPは内需と輸出に支えられ、小幅のプラス成長か 
2026年 10-12月期の実質 GDP成長率は前期比年率＋0％台前半と、小幅ながらもプラス成長
に転じると見込んでいる。 
個人消費は小幅な増加に転じるとみている。現金給与総額は前年同月比で 3％を超える伸び
が続いており、10月以降は各地で最低賃金が引き上げられていることから名目賃金の高い伸び
は続くだろう。一方、食料品等を中心に物価上昇率は高まっている。エネルギー価格について
も、資源価格高騰がラグを持って反映されることに加え、補助の一時終了や縮小も物価の押し
上げ要因となり、10-12月期の実質賃金上昇率を押し下げるだろう。設備投資は建設資材価格
と人件費の上昇が建設投資を下押しするものの、省力化投資やAI関連投資を背景に増加基調が

### Page 5

1

5 / 7

続くと見込む。 
輸出は増加に転じるとみている。米国の設備投資はAI関連需要を背景に増加が続くだろう。
米国内での設備投資増加は米国向け輸出の追い風となり、財輸出は増加すると見込んでいる。
一方、インバウンドは中国政府による日本への渡航自粛要請が続く中、増加は緩やかなものと
なるだろう。 
輸入は増加するとみている。原油輸入の回復が続くことに加え、AI関連需要を背景に電算機
類等の輸入も増加するだろう。輸入の増加が輸出の増加を上回ることで、外需の寄与度は 2四
半期連続のマイナスになると見込んでいる。 
10-12月期以降を含めたリスク要因は、AI関連需要の下振れと中東情勢の悪化である。足元
の米国経済では設備投資が景気を下支えしており、とりわけデータセンターや半導体などの IT
関連投資がけん引役となっている。AI関連サービスの世界的な利用拡大に伴い、計算能力の増
強に向けた大型投資が今後も続く可能性は高い。一方で、一部のAI関連企業では収益性や財務
面への懸念が広がっているほか、米中間選挙でAIデータセンター建設の一時停止や規制強化な
どを訴えた民主党急進左派候補者が当選するなど、AI投資を取り巻く環境には変化の兆しも見
られる。ハイパースケーラーが設備投資の拡大ペースを落とせば、半導体や情報通信機器など
の需要を下押しし、関連産業の生産減や輸出減などを通じて実体経済にも波及するだろう3。ま
た、中東情勢が悪化し、原油価格がさらに高騰すれば、物価高を通じて個人消費を一段と下押
しするだろう。 
2027年度までの経済見通しの詳細などについては、11月 20日に発表予定の「第 231回日本
経済予測」を参照されたい。

3 詳細は、畑中宏仁・神田慶司「ハイパースケーラーの投資動向が世界経済に与える影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年11月 12日）を参照。

## Document 114: 2383台光電 兆豐

Source PDF: all report pdf/2383台光電 兆豐.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-09

### Page 1

評等分級：買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅超過+30%）; 逢低買進（預期未來六個月股價漲幅介於+15%與+30%之間）; 區間操
作（預期未來六個月股價漲跌幅介於-15%與+15%之間）; 逢高賣出（預期未來六個月股價跌幅介於-15%與-30%之間）；賣出（預
期未來六個月股價跌幅超過-30%）。 
本刊所刊載之內容僅做為參考，惟已力求正確與完整，但因時間及市場客觀因素改變所造成產業、市場或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量
投資之需求與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任。任何轉載或引用本報告內容必須經本公司同意。

- 1 - 
個股報告

台光電(2383) 
Q3 財報創新高，26/27 年獲利上修 
投資評等 目標價 
買進 $ 6460 
前次投資建議：2026.08.05 
「買進」，目標價: $ 6945

報告日期：2026.11.09 研 究 員：邱岳霖

摘 要 及 調 整 原 因 
➢ 高階材料放量、報價持續上揚、新產能開出，上修 2026年 EPS至 132元 
➢ 2027年高階材料規格升級、產能持續擴增， EPS 280.8 元，考量公司在產業具有主導地位
且未來幾年將持續高速增長，不過因利率走升與市場面臨短期逆風，故下修估值，兆豐投顧
降低目標價至 6,460元(23X 27(F)PER)，潛在上漲空間 69.1%，投資評等維持「買進」。

基本資料 結論與建議 
2026/11/6 收盤價： 3820.00 ➢ 展望後市，AI Server、高速交換器與General server需
求強勁，公司持續擴產，預計2026年底月產能增至約
615萬張。AI Server規格提升帶動高階材料佔比墊高，

- 2 - 
個股報告 
資料來源：CMoney；兆豐投顧預估

營 運 分 析

台光電為全球銅箔基板龍頭廠  
台光電成立於 1992年並於 1998年市，總部位於桃園觀音，為全球最大無鹵
素銅箔基板供應商，AI Server 中 CCL 市占率超過 50%，同時是 HDI 材料
龍頭。主力產品為銅箔基板 (CCL)、黏合片 (PP)及多層壓合板 (Mass 
Lamination)，不過自 2020年起切入 IC載板材料(BT/ABF)之 CCL以作為第
二成長曲線，目前正積極推進客戶認證與產能擴充。

圖一、台光電為全球 CCL與 HDI材料龍頭廠商

資料來源：公司；兆豐投顧整理

生產基地包含台灣桃園觀音、新竹，中國江蘇昆山、廣東中山、湖北黃石，
以及馬來西亞檳城。銅箔基板月產能現況約 585 萬張，預計 2026 年底增至
約 615萬張，2027年底目標達 900萬張，且近期已與客戶敲定至 2030年產
能至 1,525 萬張。26Q3 以基礎設施應用(AI 伺服器、Switch、低軌衛星等)
為主，占比約 80%。

圖二、台光電全球布局與產能規劃

資料來源：公司；兆豐投顧整理

報價上漲紅利，M9 新產品出貨，營收與毛利率雙創高，Q3 EPS 40.5 元 
台光電(2383)公告 26Q3 財報：營收 608.85 億元，QoQ +28.8%/YoY 
+142.1%，再創歷史新高，係因報價上揚，同時 M9 開始出貨帶動所致，毛
利率 37.7%，季增 3.8ppt，年增 7.6ppt，大幅優於研究部預期之 35%，受惠
報價上漲完整反映，同時產品組合轉佳帶動，不過因持續投入新世代產品研
發帶動整體營業費用較上季增長逾 30%，稅後淨利 145 億元，QoQ 
+46.9%/YoY +265.7%，EPS 40.5元，遠優於預期之 33.5元。

- 4 - 
個股報告 
高階材料放量、報價持續上揚、新產能開出，上修 2026年 EPS至 132元 
展望後市，隨著 AI Server、高速交換器與 General server 需求持續強勁，公
司持續擴產以滿足市場需求，月產能預計 2026 年底增至約 615 萬張，季增
約 5%，此外由於 AI Server 規格持續提升，高階材料佔比將逐步墊高，同時
BT 材料已開始認證，預期年底有機會開始放量出貨；原物料方面，研究部認
為高階 CCL缺料格局將延續，同時反映原物料成本上升，預期報價有望再上
調 5-20%，此外更高階材料 M9 於 26Q3 開始出貨，預期 Q4 佔比將持續擴
大。

在整體產品組合轉佳與漲價效應帶動下，同時考量 Q3 財報遠優研究部預
期，故上修台光電26Q4營收至 700.2億元(原估593億元)，QoQ +15.0%/YoY 
+180.9%，毛利率 39%(原估 35.5%)，季增 1.3ppt，年增 10.4ppt，稅後淨
利 175.9億元，QoQ +21.3%/YoY +370.7%，EPS 49.1元(原估 37.2 元)；
同步上修 2026年稅後淨利 473億元，YoY +222.9%，EPS 132元(原估 113.2
元)。

2027年高階材料規格升級、產能持續擴增， EPS 280.8 元 
展望 2027 年，隨著 GPU 與 Switch 規格持續提升，帶動 PCB/CCL 層數與
尺寸持續提升，且 CSP持續擴大 Capex，ASIC需求亦繼續上調，目前公司
訂單能見度已達 2027 年，亦積極擴張產能，預期至 27 年底達月產能 900
萬張目標，年增近 50%。而客戶主動要求簽 LTA 並確保後續產能無虞，目
前台光電已通過至 2030 年產能建置計畫，預期將總產能大幅增加至 1,525
萬張，為現有產能的 2.5 倍，將有助於公司未來持續高速增長；此外 BT 載
板材料預計 26年底完成認證，27年開始出貨，初步規劃出貨量約 10萬張，
不過為確保未來高速成長的需求，將規劃 120萬張產能，預期將帶來新一波
成長動能。綜上所述，考量整體市場供需格局持續偏緊，且公司產能擴充積
極，輔以報價持續上升，兆豐投顧上修台光電 2027年營收至 3697.2 億元(原
估 3,209.5 億元)，YoY +75.0%，而毛利率方面，預期在新一代 GPU 推出，
同時 Switch 亦升級至 3.2T 下，M9 材料營收佔比可望由今年低個位數提升
至雙位數，在稼動率維持高檔、產品組合持續優化下，上修毛利率至 41.2%(原
估 36.6%)，年增 5.2ppt，稅後淨利 1006.2億元，YoY +112.7%，EPS 280.8
元(原估 210元)。

Update
Delta Electronics Inc.  | Asia Pacific
October  Sales +14% MoM/
+30% YoY
In this report, we focus on Delta's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• October sales came in at NT$74,666mn (+14% MoM/+30% YoY), driven by
the increasing shipment of power supply units, HVDC power racks, and liquid
cooling products.
• QTD revenue reached 35% of MSe at NT$215,881mn (+10% QoQ/+34% YoY),
and 34% of consensus at NT$219,806mn (+12% QoQ/+36% YoY).

Our view:
• We think 4Q26 revenue is tracking slightly ahead of market expectations.
• We think the slightly better Oct monthly sales should help on near-term
sentiment, though more meaningful upside to market expectations on
earnings growth might  depend on the penetration of HVDC power racks into
the coming years.
• We continue to see Delta as a key beneficiary of the increasing power
consumption of AI clusters through its comprehensive power/cooling

offerings. Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28e EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 59% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.
Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth

n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

## Document 197: 261113_daiwa_Gigabyte

Source PDF: all report pdf/261113_daiwa_Gigabyte.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Gigabyte released its 3Q26 results after market hours on 13 November, with 
net income of TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beating expectations, while revenue of 
TWD154.7bn (+7% QoQ, +94% YoY) was largely in line despite robust server demand. 
Gross margin expanded 4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% (vs. our 9.6% estimate). Management cited 
factors such as higher value-added products and better cost control, though we believe 
VGA card price hikes were the key driver. Despite strong gross margin expansion and 
operating leverage, higher profit-sharing bonuses limited OPM expansion to 2.7pp QoQ to 
8.3%, still above our/consensus estimates of 5.8%/5.9%. We expect Gigabyte to benefit 
from solid AI server demand from neoclouds as they increase power budgets and capex, 
with neoclouds and enterprises becoming key Nvidia server buyers from Vera Rubin in 
2027. Nebius reaffirmed its 5GW contracted-power and 0.8-1GW connected-power end-
2026 targets, with >1GW annual deployments from 2027; CoreWeave raised its 2026 
capex guidance to USD37-39bn, with USD132.5bn in backlog. As neoclouds serve 
sovereign and enterprise customers lacking in-house hardware capabilities, we expect 
TAM growth for server brands including Dell, Gigabyte, Lenovo and Asustek. We reaffirm 
our AI server revenue forecasts of TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E. We 
expect the AI server mix to dilute the GM to 8.9% in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E. We 
reiterate our Buy (1) rating with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on 15x the 1-year-forward 
diluted EPS; key downside risk is Nvidia’s business model change.

Gigabyte’s 3Q26 results highlights 
(TWDm) Actual QoQ% YoY% Daiwa Diff % Consensus Diff %

Gross profit 21,661 56% 175% 15,176 43% 16,010 35% 
  Gross margin (%) 14.0%   9.6%  10.3%

Source: Company data, Daiwa forecasts, Bloomberg

What’s the impact: 
Below are our read-throughs from Gigabyte’s 3Q26 results (released on 13 November).

• 3Q26 results first-take: profitability beat expectations. Gigabyte’s net income of 
TWD8.57bn (EPS: TWD12.07) beat our/Bloomberg consensus estimates by 32%/29%, 
respectively. Revenue (TWD154.7bn; up 7% QoQ and 94% YoY) was largely in-line with 
estimates, despite robust server demand. Nevertheless, the gross margin expanded by 
4.4pp QoQ to 14.0% in 3Q26 (exceeding our estimate of 9.6%). Management attributed 
the strength to an improved product mix, higher value-added products, strategic 
procurement, and better cost control, but we believe price hikes in VGA cards to be the 
key factor. Despite strong gross margin expansion and operating leverage, higher profit-
sharing bonus expenses caused the operating margin to only rise by 2.7pp QoQ to 8.3% 
(still beating our/street’s estimates of 5.8%/5.9%).

• Business outlook. We believe Gigabyte will ride on consistently solid AI server demand 
from the neoclouds, as key vendors continue to raise their power budget and capex. 
This aligns with our view that the neoclouds and enterprise will gradually become key

buyers of Nvidia AI servers starting from the Vera Rubin generation in 2027. Nebius 
(NBIS US, NR) reaffirmed its end-2026 contracted-power target of 5GW (up from >4GW 
currently contracted) and connected-power target of 0.8-1GW, with plans to deploy 
>1GW annually starting in 2027. Meanwhile, Coreweave (CRWV US, NR) raised its 
2026 capex guidance to USD37-39bn (from USD35-39bn) with an order backlog of 
USD132.5bn (vs. USD3.7bn revenue in 3Q26). We believe the neoclouds will become a 
key computing source for sovereign, enterprise and other corporate customers that 
cannot design and maintain the hardware themselves. As such, we expect the TAM for 
server brands like Dell (DELL US, USD371.17, Outperform [2], covered by Louis 
Miscioscia), Gigabyte, Lenovo (992 HK, NR) and Asustek (2357 TT, NR) to rise.

While management expects continued revenue growth and improving operating 
leverage in 2027, we reaffirm our AI server revenue assumptions for Gigabyte of 
TWD412bn in 2026E and TWD565bn in 2027E (vs. TWD180bn in 2025). We also 
believe the rising contribution from AI servers will be structurally margin-dilutive, despite 
potential benefits from higher value-added solutions, strategic procurement, and 
economies of scale. We therefore forecast gross margin to gradually normalise to 8.9% 
in 2027E and 8.4% in 2028E (vs. 14.0% in 3Q26).

What we recommend: 
We have a Buy (1) rating on Gigabyte, with a 12M TP of TWD490, based on a target PER 
of 15x, applied to our 1-year-forward diluted EPS. Key downside risk: earnings impact from 
Nvidia’s business model change.

hyeonseo.bae@meritz.co.kr 배당은 지켜질 것  
3Q26 지배주주 순이익, 컨센서스 -38% 하회 
매출 31.6조원 (+10% YoY)으로 컨센서스에 부합했다. 출고 판매 (+10% YoY), 소
매 판매 (+8% YoY) 모두 높은 성장을 기록하며, 외형 확대에 기여했다. 
영업이익 1.82조원 (+24% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -25% 하회했다. 전년 동기 대미 
관세 25% 부과에 따른 기저효과 +5,330억원 (2025년 11월부터 15%로 하향)이 
존재했고 기말 환율 (원/달러 -12% QoQ 강세) 변동에 따른 판매보증 충당부채 환
입 +6,700억원을 인식했음에도 불구하고, 신규 출시 저가 BEV 모델들의 판매 확대
에 따른 전사 평균 판매가격 하락  할인 경쟁 심화  원재료 및 운송비 상승으로 부
진한 실적을 기록했다. 
지배주주 순이익 1.29조원 (-10% YoY)으로 컨센서스를 -38% 하회했다. 영업 실적 
부진과 함께 원화 강세에 의한 외화자산 평가손실 및 관계사 실적 악화에 따른 지분
법 이익 감소 (-36% YoY)가 더해진 결과다.

6,000원 이상 배당 유지 전망 
4Q26 이후 실적 방향도 먹구름이 가득하다. 원/달러 환율의 YoY 강세 반영이 시작
된다. 미국 시장 내 경쟁력 확보를 위해 가장 중요한 원/엔 환율의 방향성 또한 비우
호적 흐름이 지속 중이다. 내수 시장을 포함해 전세계 주요 시장 내 중국산 차량과의 
경쟁 강도도 높아지고 있다. 원유 가격 뿐만 아니라 알루미늄, 구리 등 핵심 원재료 
가격의 상승 반영도 이어지고 있다. 
2026년 연간 지배주주 순이익 6.9조원 (-9% YoY)을 전망한다. 주주환원 총액은 축
소될 것이다. 다만 자사주 매입을 줄이더라도 배당은 전년 수준을 지킬 것으로 기대
한다. 참고로 2025년 실적에 기반한 주주환원은 자사주 매입 0.9조원, 배당 2.6조원 
(주당 6,800원)이었으며, 주주환원율은 48%였다. 현대차그룹은 Boston Dynamics
에 대한 증자를 이어가고 있다. 정의선 회장도 마찬가지다. 그리고 기아의 배당은 현
대차  현대글로비스의 배당과 함께 증자 재원으로 활용되고 있다. 6,000원 이상의 
배당 유지를 기대하며, 6% 이상 시가 배당 수익률 제시를 통해 주가 하방 경직성 확
보가 가능하다고 판단한다. 기업가치 상승 재개를 위해서는 기존 차량 제조  판매 사
업을 넘어 피지컬 데이터 디바이스 제공 사업 진출 가시성 확보가 필요하다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 11. 2

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 251: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%)과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%)을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원

## Document 269: [207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97

Source PDF: all report pdf/[207940] 삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대삼성바이오로직스(207940) 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 기대단기 부담_2a8cdc97.pdf

투자의견 BUY  
매수, 유지

목표주가 2,000,000 
유지  
현재주가 
(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI.
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy.
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 5

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

## Document 370: FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Outlook 2027_ Seven key calls.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

We expect 2027 to be a low-volatility year in FX, with central
banks and rate diﬀerentials ﬁrmly in the driving seat. We see a
dollar decline resuming from 2Q27, while diverging monetary,
growth and external dynamics create opportunities across
regions

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole, Frantisek Taborsky and Deepali
Bhargava
Winners and losers in FX next year

### Page 2

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Not a breakout year for volatility
Our most important FX call for 2027 is the market environment. Our
macro-driven call is that lower oil prices and only a modest
recalibration in global monetary policy mean that FX volatility can
stay low. This is not 2022/2023, when major central banks
belatedly delivered 500bp tightening cycles to deal with double-
digit inﬂation. If 2026 has shown us anything, it is that FX markets
can prove quite resilient in this high-growth, high-inﬂation global

economy. And markets have proved quite resilient to geopolitical
risk as well.
For the majority of the last ﬁve years, the market has
overestimated FX volatility. There may be some structural factors at
play here, such as huge growth in ﬁnancial assets bringing in more
liquidity to the FX market and the greater use of FX pricing algos on
the buy-side, limiting the market fallout when trading conditions
thin.
Yet market volatility has not vanished. There are plenty of risks out

there, which we highlight in a dedicated section below. The same
applies for market liquidity. If the shock is big enough – such as
Liberation Day tariﬀs in 2025 or the start of the Iran war this year –
market depth can fall away quickly. The prospect of fat tails, low-
probability, high-impact event risks, is certainly a threat to global
FX markets. But the bar for these risks to raise volatility is a high
one.
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.

You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.

Targeting Cookies

### Page 3

Volatility has mostly undershot expectations
in 2026
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Look no further than central banks in
G10
In a year dominated by geopolitical risk and energy shock, currency
investors quickly moved beyond headline trading and increasingly
looked to short-term rate diﬀerentials as the primary guide for
currencies. We expect that to remain the case next year.
Central bank tightening is moving beyond its “insurance” phase,
requiring evidence that inﬂationary pressures are becoming

persistent. Oil prices will remain part of the story, but we think the
more important distinction is between economies where the shock
can generate more lasting inﬂation and those where it cannot. On
top of that, the central banks’ FX-speciﬁc approach will be key.
Remember how, in 2022-2024, the fear of ampliﬁed inﬂation
eﬀects from a weak currency prompted tighter ﬁscal policies
among some often dovish-leaning central banks.
Here are our top picks among G10 currencies for next year:

1. JPY – the best risk/reward. We don’t have a huge
outperformance of the yen in our forecasts, especially relative
to the euro. But that’s primarily due to our assumption that the
euro will drop its French-related risk premium by end-2028, and
Your privacy is important
At ING we respect your right to choose how your data is used.
You can review and change your preferences at any time in
our Cookie policy.
Essential
Performance Cookies
Targeting Cookies

### Page 4

a carry-favourable environment can somewhat contain yen
gains. However, we think the yen oﬀers the best risk-reward
proﬁle in G10. Alongside being sharply undervalued based on
long-term fundamentals, it faces limited risks of dovish
repricing as the Bank of Japan’s tightening cycle predated the
Iran war. It incidentally has strong safe-haven value against
some fat-tail risks (AI bust, ﬁscal dominance, dollar
debasement, some geopolitical events) and stands to be a

beneﬁciary of an ultimate capitulation in USD rates. Downside
risks are, crucially, limited by a coordinated Tokyo-Washington
eﬀort to prevent sharp USD/JPY rallies.
2. CHF – SNB turning pro-franc. If 2027 is the year when global
interest rates peak and then reverse, the opportunity cost of
holding the franc starts to fall. We also think the Swiss National
Bank will prove more tolerant of franc strength. Not only are
there fewer complaints about the strong franc from local

corporates, but Switzerland’s inﬂation is much lower than
trading partners and the SNB might again opt for nominal
franc appreciation to keep the real exchange rate stable.
3. NZD – The high-beta gamble. Among higher-beta currencies,
we see good potential for the New Zealand dollar next year. The
Reserve Bank of New Zealand has delivered a few dovish
surprises, but we think it is underestimating the inﬂation risks
for 2027: we expect at least two hikes, also helped by a fairly

robust growth backdrop. NZD should be favoured against the
Australian dollar to play a decline in global rates, as the
Reserve Bank of Australia may already shift to cuts in the third
quarter of 2027.
Sterling, dollar and AUD face largest dovish
repricing risk

### Page 5

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Dollar decline is delayed, not cancelled
Our baseline for global inﬂation in 2027 remains benign, leaving
most of our major central bank calls more dovish than current
market pricing. We expect the Federal Reserve to deliver one ﬁnal
hike in December 2026, taking rates to a 4.25% peak, but we don’t
think the additional hikes priced by 3Q27 will materialise. Modest
second-round eﬀects should fail to sustain inﬂation once the 2Q27

base-eﬀect decline comes through. Importantly, and unlike in other
economies, we think benign inﬂation rather than weaker growth
will be the main catalyst for a dovish Fed turn. That means markets
may be reluctant to price a large Fed cutting cycle too early, but it’s
equally an environment where we envision a Fed cut in 4Q27.
Ultimately, we expect a nearly 100bp drop in the 2-year USD swap
rate by the end of 2027, with most of the move concentrated in the

year’s second half.
We expect the dollar to weaken alongside that repricing, with
greater conviction in the downside story later in the year. The Fed
should remain cautious about turning dovish before clearer
evidence of disinﬂation emerges, while elevated funding rates will
continue to make it expensive to sell or hedge dollars until some
easing is priced in. And because we also hold dovish views on other
central banks, we don’t expect narrower USD short-term rate

diﬀerentials to trigger a particularly large dollar downturn. We
target a roughly 5% decline in DXY to 97 by end-2027.

Front-end rates to drive DXY lower
Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Bond premium on the euro needs bad
news fuel
Our colleagues in the macro and rates team remain cautious about
a recovery in French sentiment in the next couple of quarters. Weak
growth (0.5% in 2027) should continue to make ﬁscal arithmetic
complicated, and we forecast a deﬁcit of 5.5% next year –
assuming a budget is passed – above the 5.0% government target.
Election frontrunner Marine Le Pen has signalled large spending

cuts, although their credibility remains in question. She is, anyway,
a more market-friendly candidate than left-wing rival Jean-Luc
Mélenchon.
We think markets will be wary of jumping into a meaningful OAT
relief rally before the presidential election (18 April-2 May) and likely
parliamentary elections in the summer are out of the way. We are
not forecasting another big selloﬀ in French bonds but think 125-
150bp will be the norm for the OAT-Bund 10yr spread into 2Q27,

before some tightening towards 100bp in 2H27.
The euro has been embedding a French risk premium since early
October, meaning a further material deterioration in sentiment
would probably be needed to generate additional idiosyncratic
pressure. Historically, the link between euro-area sovereign spreads

Source: ING, Reﬁnitiv
Central Europe: Same shocks, different
paths for local FX
The Central Europe region is entering a year of divergence. The
Czech Republic, Poland and Hungary face similar risks from energy
prices, geopolitics and volatile global markets, but increasingly
diﬀerent monetary, ﬁscal and growth dynamics mean CEE3 is
becoming less of a single FX trade.

In the Czech Republic, the Czech National Bank has already
delivered two hikes, taking rates to 4%, and remains the most
hawkish central bank in the region. If inﬂation proves more
persistent, further tightening remains possible. Positive nominal
and real rate diﬀerentials and high credibility should therefore keep
the koruna relatively defensive during periods of global uncertainty,

### Page 8

while any easing of external risks could put it back on an

Poland is moving diﬀerently. The National Bank of Poland is still
waiting to deliver its ﬁrst hike, leaving the zloty with less monetary
support for now. Strong growth and sizeable EU inﬂows remain
important anchors, but narrower rate diﬀerentials, loose ﬁscal
policy and rising political and rating risks have weakened PLN’s
relative appeal. We therefore see the zloty as more vulnerable to

higher global yields, energy prices and geopolitical risks.
Hungary is heading in the opposite direction, with the National
Bank of Hungary having cut rates in recent months. We expect
another cut only later next year, provided global conditions allow it.
Attractive carry, EU-fund inﬂows and an improving external balance
should support the forint, while progress towards euro adoption
provides another positive longer-term story. The central bank’s

focus on the exchange rate should meanwhile limit the pace of
easing and keep EUR/HUF biased towards the stronger end of its
range.

### Page 9

A softer dollar should provide a broad tailwind for Asian FX,
particularly in the second half of 2027, but we expect performance
to remain highly diﬀerentiated. We favour the Korean won and
Taiwanese dollar, expect further renminbi appreciation – albeit with
greater two-way volatility – and see continued downside risks for
the Indonesian rupiah and Philippine peso. We are more
constructive on the Indian rupee.
Current account strength should continue to favour the tech

currencies. Although the AI cycle may be plateauing, activity
remains high and strong export revenues should support the KRW
and TWD. Exporter FX conversion and measures encouraging the
repatriation of overseas assets should provide additional support.
CNY should also beneﬁt from strong external balances, although
we expect a less one-directional appreciation trend.
Rate support will become less favourable. Most Asian central banks
are further advanced in their tightening cycles than the Fed and

should ﬁnish hiking by early 2027 before preparing for easing.
Narrowing rate support should particularly weigh on the rate-
sensitive IDR. Continued household and corporate demand for
foreign assets could also oﬀset some of the beneﬁts from strong
current accounts.
Lower oil and food prices should help importers in the second half
of 2027, but capital ﬂows are unlikely to recover evenly. Political
uncertainty and the MSCI downgrade should also remain

headwinds for the IDR, while weaker FDI inﬂows leave the PHP
vulnerable. By contrast, we favour the INR, where resilient growth,
supply-chain diversiﬁcation and continued FDI should provide
support, while leaving India less exposed to swings in the AI cycle.

### Page 10

Source: ING, Macrobond
Latam: Banxico’s neglect of the peso
stands out
We’ve said that central bank policy is going to be more important
than ever in 2027. And exchange rates can be an important part of
the monetary toolkit – especially in small, open economies. In a
world where central bank tightening cycles are being priced more
hawkishly, Banxico’s unabashedly dovish stance stands out.
When cutting rates to 6.25% in November, Banxico noted that,

because of slack economic conditions, the pass-through of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations to consumer prices would be limited.
The IMF puts that economic ‘slack’ – or the negative output gap – at
0.4% of GDP in 2027. That makes Banxico far less concerned about
a weaker currency than most other central banks.
In addition, Banxico has now cut the peso’s policy rate protection to
just 225bp over the Fed. That is a record low and leaves the peso
vulnerable to any deterioration in the external environment. We see

the peso as a relative underperformer in 2027.
Further south, the Brazilian real has been causing a lot of pain to
investors recently. The fear is that President Luiz Inácio Lula da
Silva’s pre-election giveaways will come home to roost next year.
His choice of Finance Minister is going to be important, as will any
progress on a 2027 budget. Preventing a bigger sell-oﬀ in the real,
however, is the independent central bank running very high real

調降評等 
中立(Neutral) 
展望 2027年營運正向，雙位數成長可期  
⚫ 3Q26毛利率低於預期，磷化銦產品比重下滑 
⚫ 2027年基板供應將改善，磷化銦產品仍為重要成長動能 
⚫ 砷化鎵光電產品持續開發，有望成為新營運動能 
3Q26財報低於預期：公司於 2026年 11月 12日舉行第三季法說會，
3Q26 營收 3.19 億元(QoQ+24.64%，YoY+26.84%)、毛利率 38.72% 
(QoQ+4.72ppts，YoY+7.05ppts)、營業利益率 18.16 %(QoQ+9.73ppts，
YoY+4.75ppts)、稅後淨利 5,933 萬元(QoQ+214%，YoY+98%)、單季
1.26元 EPS。3Q26營收及毛利率低於我們預期，主要因磷化銦基板供
應仍受限制，德系基板供應商產能爬坡進展不如預期，而主要日系供
應商供貨份額未有太大改變，使高毛利磷化銦產品營收佔比自前季
48.7%下滑至 33.4%，且砷化鎵產品營收占比因相關射頻及國防訂單顯
著上升，影響產品組合。3Q26營業費用 0.65億元大約符合我們預期，
因公司持續投注研發費用於專業機台進行較高階產品的研發，費用較
往年明顯提升。 
磷化銦磊晶片仍是 2027年主要成長動能：2027年成長動能仍來自磷
化銦磊晶片，雖 3Q26供應狀況未如我們先前預期，公司認為磷化銦
基板供應於 4Q26已有些微放緩跡象，展望 2027年供應限制有望進一
步改善，預期毛利率將回升至四成以上水準。2027年公司展望營收成
長約 10%至 25%，新興 APD及 HBT磊晶產品也預計於 2027年量產，
而除磷化銦外，4Q26起砷化鎵及銻化鎵國防與商用訂單顯著成長，其
中銻化鎵紅外磊晶片已加速訂單需求，預期 2027 年訂單持續擴大，
並增加產能。磷化銦基板供應未如先前所預期顯著改善以及公司明年
展望狀況，我們下修 2027年營收及獲利預估。 
砷化鎵光電產品靜待多點開花：為降低對傳統磷化銦基板供應的依
賴，公司積極開發 QD Laser、變晶型 PIN等新產品切入光通訊市場，
其中，QD Laser 已受到客戶初步良好反饋，進行送樣當中。 200G 
VCSEL方面也持續進行研發，並已與客戶配合 NRE。 
受股數稀釋獲利及市場影響下調評價：公司與光聖進行股份交換使流
通在外股數增加致流通在外股數上升，我們預估 2026、2027年 EPS 
分別為 5.08、7.66元，此外，考量近期市場評價有較大幅度修正，較
前次報告下調本益比，我們基於 2027預估 EPS x 50倍本益比，目標
價為 383元，調降至中立之投資評等建議。風險因子：基板供應短缺。

## Document 425: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-16).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.
